{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WNqcK3RGcGCpJLnr4y3JM8","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Glenwood Systems LLC — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-16\n     4→Developer website: http://www.glenwoodsystems.com\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Glenwood Systems LLC is a privately held healthcare IT company headquartered in Waterbury, Connecticut. Founded in 1998 (now 27+ years in operation), the company provides EHR, practice management, revenue cycle management, and related services to ambulatory medical practices, surgical centers, and hospitals across the U.S. The company reports approximately $17.3 million in revenue and 162 employees (per Dun & Bradstreet/ZoomInfo). Their website claims \"hundreds of satisfied clients,\" though no precise customer count is published.\n     9→\n    10→Glenwood Systems offers a suite of products under the \"Glace\" brand: GlaceEMR (the certified EHR), GlaceRCM (revenue cycle management/billing services), GlaceOffice (practice administration tools), GlaceScribe (AI-powered transcription using Amazon HealthScribe), GlaceGrow (digital marketing for patient acquisition), GlacePhoneSmart (automated phone/task handling), and GlaceBillSmart (AI billing automation). The company primarily targets small-to-mid-size ambulatory practices and positions itself as an all-in-one solution combining clinical, billing, and administrative functions.\n    11→\n    12→## Product: GlaceEMR\n    13→\n    14→CHPL ID: 9559\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→GlaceEMR is a cloud-based, ONC-certified electronic health records system that combines clinical documentation with practice management capabilities. It is certified as a Complete EHR (per the CHPL product number prefix 15.04.04) with extensive certification across 40+ criteria spanning clinical data (a)(1)-(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)-(15); transitions of care (b)(1)-(3), (b)(7)-(9); patient portal (e)(1), (e)(3); public health reporting (f)(1)-(2), (f)(4)-(5), (f)(7); CQMs (c)(1)-(4); and FHIR API access (g)(7), (g)(9)-(10). The intended users are \"Outpatient Clinic\" staff per the CHPL metadata.\n    19→\n    20→GlaceEMR is the core clinical module, but it is part of the larger Glace product ecosystem. The certified module appears to encompass both the clinical EHR and integrated practice management/billing functionality — the transparency disclosure describes it as including \"clinical documentation, eRX, patient portal, hosting, and maintenance\" in the base package, with billing/RCM services available as add-ons (GlacePremium and GlaceComplete tiers). The product is offered in multiple service tiers: GlaceBasic, GlacePremium, GlacePremium+, GlaceComplete, and GlaceEnterprise.\n    21→\n    22→### Users & Market\n    23→\n    24→GlaceEMR targets ambulatory outpatient practices across multiple specialties. The vendor's marketing specifically calls out podiatry, psychiatry/behavioral health, internal medicine, urgent care, cardiology, neurology, ophthalmology, rheumatology, and general surgery. The behavioral health module is particularly detailed, suggesting it's an important vertical. The vendor also mentions serving surgical centers and hospitals, though the primary market appears to be ambulatory practices.\n    25→\n    26→End users include physicians (MDs/DOs), nurse practitioners, practice managers, office managers, and billing staff. The system has a patient portal for patient-facing access as well. The product is cloud-based, accessible across devices including a smartphone app for mobile access.\n    27→\n    28→No specific customer counts are published, but the vendor claims \"hundreds of satisfied clients\" and 24+ years of operation. Pricing starts at $54/month per provider for the base EMR, scaling up to $1,000/month for GlaceBasic, with GlacePremium/GlaceComplete tiers using a revenue-sharing model (percentage of collected revenue or monthly minimum, whichever is greater).\n    29→\n    30→### Modules & Functionality\n    31→\n    32→Based on vendor materials, product pages, the transparency disclosure, and third-party review sites, GlaceEMR includes the following modules and capabilities:\n    33→\n    34→**Clinical Documentation**\n    35→- Hundreds of pre-built specialty-specific templates (customizable)\n    36→- AI-integrated documentation assistance including scribing, dictation-to-form, auto-coding, and document generation (powered in part by Amazon HealthScribe via GlaceScribe)\n    37→- Custom templates, pre-filled reusable templates, shortcuts, and order-sets\n    38→- Clinical decision support with data summarization and analytics\n    39→\n    40→**Scheduling**\n    41→- Integrated appointment scheduler managing clinical slots across multiple resources\n    42→- Multi-channel appointment reminder system (reduces no-shows)\n    43→- Group therapy scheduler (behavioral health)\n    44→- Telehealth appointment scheduling\n    45→\n    46→**E-Prescribing**\n    47→- Electronic prescriptions via SureScripts integration\n    48→- EPCS (Electronic Prescribing of Controlled Substances) with two-way authentication and biometric verification\n    49→- PMP (Prescription Drug Monitoring Program) support\n    50→- Pharmacy refill request handling\n    51→- Multum Lexi-Data integration for drug data\n    52→\n    53→**Patient Portal**\n    54→- Two-way patient-provider communication/messaging\n    55→- Appointment scheduling\n    56→- Prescription refill requests\n    57→- Lab results viewing\n    58→- Contactless check-in and KIOSK functionality\n    59→\n    60→**Lab Integration**\n    61→- \"Glenwood standard lab interface program\" for lab order/result exchange\n    62→- Medical device interfaces\n    63→- Integration with partner healthcare entities\n    64→\n    65→**Billing & Practice Management**\n    66→- Integrated billing and coding module for electronic claims submission\n    67→- ICD-10 and ICD-9 code support\n    68→- Two-way data flow between clinical and billing modules (\"cleaner billing claim submission information for improved claim collection results\")\n    69→- Charge capture integrated with clinical documentation\n    70→- Patient balance collection (eBills, text reminders, patient portal, kiosk terminals)\n    71→- Eligibility verification\n    72→\n    73→**Revenue Cycle Management (add-on tiers)**\n    74→- GlacePremium and GlaceComplete offer full RCM billing services\n    75→- GlaceRCM handles charge capture through claim resolution\n    76→- Denial management with pattern-learning software\n    77→- A/R tracking and management\n    78→- Analytics and transparent reporting dashboards\n    79→- Accepts charges from GlaceEMR, third-party EMRs, mobile devices, and rounding software\n    80→\n    81→**Telehealth**\n    82→- Comprehensive telemedicine module from scheduling through billing\n    83→- Video call/conferencing functionality\n    84→- Virtual receptionist\n    85→\n    86→**Behavioral Health (specialty module)**\n    87→- Group therapy management and scheduling\n    88→- Mental Status Exam, Advanced Treatment Plan, screening templates\n    89→- PHQ-9, BIMS, Mini Mental Status Exam, suicidal assessment protocols\n    90→- DSM-5 assessments\n    91→- Addiction management templates\n    92→- Treatment plan and goal tracking with objective benchmarks\n    93→- Case manager resource allocation analysis\n    94→\n    95→**Quality Reporting & Compliance**\n    96→- MIPS/MACRA capture and dashboard\n    97→- PCMH and ACO program support\n    98→- Meaningful Use attestation support\n    99→- Clinical quality measure (CQM) reporting\n   100→- Care Coordination Management (CCM) module\n   101→- Remote Patient Monitoring (RPM) module\n   102→- Public health reporting (immunization registries, syndromic surveillance, cancer/specialized registries)\n   103→\n   104→**Practice Administration (via GlaceOffice add-on)**\n   105→- HR management, recruiting, credentialing\n   106→- Payroll administration, bookkeeping (AP/AR/reconciliation)\n   107→- Payment gateway for patient payments\n   108→- Email and fax routing/tracking\n   109→- Internet and telephony systems\n   110→- HIPAA and security audits\n   111→\n   112→### Data & Content\n   113→\n   114→Based on the features and modules described across vendor materials and reviews, GlaceEMR stores and manages the following categories of data:\n   115→\n   116→**Clinical data**: Patient demographics, clinical encounter documentation (via specialty templates), problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists, vital signs, clinical notes, assessments (including behavioral health screening instruments like PHQ-9, BIMS, Mini Mental Status Exam), treatment plans with goals and progress tracking, immunization records, growth charts.\n   117→\n   118→**Orders and results**: Lab orders and results (via lab interface program), medication orders/prescriptions (via SureScripts e-prescribing), PDMP data, pharmacy refill requests.\n   119→\n   120→**Scheduling data**: Appointment records, provider schedules, resource allocation, group therapy schedules, appointment reminders and no-show tracking.\n   121→\n   122→**Messaging and communication**: Patient portal messages (two-way), secure messaging, patient engagement communications, appointment reminders.\n   123→\n   124→**Billing and financial data**: Claims/charge data, ICD-10/ICD-9 codes, CPT codes (implied by charge capture and auto-coding), insurance eligibility data, accounts receivable, patient balances, denial records, payment records. The extent of billing data stored within GlaceEMR itself vs. the add-on GlaceRCM service is somewhat unclear — the base product includes billing/coding for claims submission, while the GlacePremium/GlaceComplete tiers add full RCM services.\n   125→\n   126→**Documents**: Clinical documents, care coordination documents (CCDs for transitions of care, given (b)(1)-(3) certification), fax records (via GlaceOffice integration).\n   127→\n   128→**Quality/reporting data**: CQM data, MIPS/MACRA performance metrics, public health reporting data (immunizations, syndromic surveillance, cancer registries — per (f)(1),(2),(4),(5),(7) certification).\n   129→\n   130→**Telehealth data**: Telehealth visit records and video conferencing session data.\n   131→\n   132→**Audit/system data**: Authentication and access logs (per (d) criteria certification), data integrity records.\n   133→\n   134→**What's less clear**: The boundary between GlaceEMR and GlaceOffice/GlaceRCM for administrative and financial data. The vendor describes GlaceOffice as handling HR, payroll, bookkeeping, and credentialing — whether that data resides in the same system as GlaceEMR or is separately managed is not explicitly stated. Similarly, GlaceRCM accepts charges from \"third-party EMRs\" as well, suggesting it may be a somewhat independent service, but it also deeply integrates with GlaceEMR.\n   135→\n   136→---\n   137→\n   138→## Broader Product Ecosystem\n   139→\n   140→Glenwood Systems markets several companion products alongside GlaceEMR:\n   141→\n   142→- **GlaceRCM**: Full revenue cycle management service. Can work with GlaceEMR or third-party EMRs. Manages the complete billing lifecycle from eligibility verification through denial management and patient collections.\n   143→- **GlaceOffice**: Practice administration suite covering HR, credentialing, payroll, bookkeeping, payment processing, and IT infrastructure management.\n   144→- **GlaceScribe**: AI transcription/scribe service powered by Amazon HealthScribe, integrated into GlaceEMR.\n   145→- **GlaceGrow**: Digital marketing services for patient acquisition (SEO, social media, reputation management).\n   146→- **GlacePhoneSmart**: Automated phone answering and routine task handling.\n   147→- **GlaceBillSmart**: AI automation for billing tasks, denials, and A/R management.\n   148→\n   149→These products appear to be marketed as separate add-ons/services rather than components of the certified health IT module, though GlaceRCM and GlaceScribe integrate deeply with GlaceEMR. For EHI export purposes, the key question is what data resides within the GlaceEMR system boundary vs. in these companion services.\n   150→\n   151→## User Sentiment\n   152→\n   153→Third-party reviews on emrsystems.net, FindEMR, and other sites show mixed reception (roughly 3/5 overall). Users praise the system's template library, customizability, integrated workflows, and ease of record access. Criticisms focus on system slowness during peak hours, an outdated-looking interface, occasional freezing, and inconsistent customer support responsiveness. Multiple reviews describe the system as comprehensive but sometimes clunky.\n   154→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. features and modules described across vendor materials and reviews, GlaceEMR stores and manages the following categories of data:\n\n**Clinical data**: Patient demographics, clinical encounter documentation (via specialty templates), problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists, vital signs, clinical notes, assessments (including behavioral health screening instruments like PHQ-9, BIMS, Can work with GlaceEMR or third-party EMRs. Manages the complete billing lifecycle from eligibility verification through denial management and patient collections.\n- **GlaceOffice**: Practice administration suite covering HR, credentialing, payroll, bookkeeping, payment processing, and IT infrastructure management.\n- **GlaceScribe**: AI transcription/scribe service powered by Amazon HealthScribe, integrated into GlaceEMR.\n- **GlaceGrow**: Digital marketing services for patient acquisition (SEO, social media, reputation management).\n- **GlacePhoneSmart**: Automated phone answering and routine task handling.\n- **GlaceBillSmart**: AI automation for billing tasks, denials, and A/R management.\n\nThese products appear to be marketed as separate add-ons/services rather than components of the certified health IT module, though GlaceRCM and GlaceScribe integrate deeply with GlaceEMR. Users praise the system's template library, customizability, integrated workflows, and ease of record access. * 100) + ')';\n   259→                  c.style.height = h + (d * f) + 'px'\n   260→                }\n   261→                return {\n   262→                  dd: dd\n   263→                }\n   264→              }();\n   265→            </script>\n   266→            <script type=\"text/javascript\">\n   267→              var gaJsHost = ((\"https:\" == document.location.protocol) ? 2023\n\n4|Page\n\n\fIntroduction\nElectronic Health Information (EHI) export will enable a clinician to export all EHI of a single patient or\npopulation of patients or all their patients stored in the GlaceEMR.\nEHI means “electronic protected health information” (ePHI) as defined in 45 CFR 160.103 to the extent\nthat it would be included in a designated record set as defined in 45 CFR 164.501, regardless of whether\nthe group of records are used or maintained by or for the practice.\n\nSingle Patient Export\nEnable a user to timely create an export file with all the EHI of a single patient that are stored in\nGlaceEMR. Single Patient EHI Export includes the capability for an authorized user to execute a single\npatient export and download the zip file contain all the EHI of this exported patient.\n\nPatient Population Export\nEnable a user to timely create an export file with all the EHI belong to a selected group of patients or\nentire patient population that are stored in GlaceEMR. Patient Population EHI Export includes the\ncapability for an authorized user to either to specify a criteria to select a population of patients or all the\npatients in the database to export and download the zip file contain all the EHI of this exported patients.\n\nContents of the Exported Data\nOnce the export is completed a link to a zip file will be presented. description: string }[];\n  };\n  file_schemas: FileSchema[];\n  extraction_stats: {\n    total_files_documented: number;\n    total_columns_documented: number;\n    files_with_primary_keys: number;\n    files_with_foreign_keys: number;\n  };\n}\n\nconst text = readFileSync(INPUT, \"utf-8\");\nconst lines = text.split(\"\\n\");\n\n// ── helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nfunction clean(s: string): string {\n  return s.replace(/\\s+/g, \" \").trim();\n}\n\n// The pdftotext output has a specific pattern for each file schema.\n// We'll use a state-machine approach to parse sections.\n\n// First, manually define the known files and their schemas based on the\n// well-structured PDF. We parse section by section.\n\nfunction parseSchemas(): FileSchema[] {\n  const schemas: FileSchema[] = [];\n\n  // Find all \"Filename:\" markers\n  const filenamePattern = /^Filename:\\s*(.+)/;\n  const contentsPattern = /^Contents:\\s*(.+)/;\n\n  let i = 0;\n  while (i < lines.length) {\n    const line = clean(lines[i]);\n\n    const fnMatch = line.match(filenamePattern);\n    if (!fnMatch) {\n      i++;\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    const filename = fnMatch[1].trim();\n    const folder = filename.split(\"/\")[0];\n    i++;\n\n    // Gather contents description (may span multiple lines)\n    let contentsDesc = \"\";\n    while (i < lines.length) {\n      const cl = clean(lines[i]);\n      if (!cl || cl.match(/^\\d+\\s*\\|\\s*P\\s*a\\s*g\\s*e/) || cl.match(/^Data Export/)) {\n        i++;\n        continue;\n      }\n      const cm = cl.match(contentsPattern);\n      if (cm) {\n        contentsDesc = cm[1];\n        i++;\n        // Continue gathering if next lines are continuation\n        while (i < lines.length) {\n          const nl = clean(lines[i]);\n          if (!nl || nl === \"Schema:\" || nl.match(/^Column Name/) || nl.match(/^\\d+\\s*\\|\\s*P\\s*a\\s*g\\s*e/) || nl.match(/^Data Export/)) break;\n          contentsDesc += \" \" + nl;\n          i++;\n        }\n        break;\n      }\n      break;\n    }\n\n    // Skip to \"Schema:\" or \"Column Name\"\n    while (i < lines.length) {\n      const cl = clean(lines[i]);\n      if (cl === \"Schema:\" || cl.match(/^Column Name/)) break;\n      i++;\n    }\n\n    // Skip \"Schema:\" line\n    if (clean(lines[i]) === \"Schema:\") i++;\n\n    // Skip \"Column Name\" header line and the \"Data Type\" / \"Description\" / \"Comments\" header\n    // The pdftotext output splits the 4-column table headers across lines\n    while (i < lines.length) {\n      const cl = clean(lines[i]);\n      if (cl === \"Column Name\" || cl === \"Data Type\" || cl === \"Description\" || cl === \"Comments\") {\n        i++;\n        continue;\n      }\n      break;\n    }\n\n    // Now parse columns. The pattern from pdftotext is:\n    // Column Name on its own line, then Data Type, then Description, then Comments\n    // Some values span multiple lines. We need to detect when a new column starts.\n    const columns: Column[] = [];\n    let currentCol: Partial<Column> | null = null;\n\n    // Collect all remaining lines until the next \"Filename:\" or end of known section\n    const columnLines: string[] = [];\n    while (i < lines.length) {\n      const cl = clean(lines[i]);\n      // Stop at next filename or at end markers\n      if (cl.match(/^Filename:/) || cl.match(/^PMS File Schemas/) || cl.match(/^EMR File Schemas/)) break;\n      // Skip page markers and data export footers\n      if (cl.match(/^\\d+\\s*\\|\\s*P\\s*a\\s*g\\s*e/) || cl.match(/^Data Export \\/ Version/)) {\n        i++;\n        continue;\n      }\n      columnLines.push(cl);\n      i++;\n    }\n\n    // Parse the column lines using knowledge of the data types that appear\n    const dataTypes = [\n      \"Integer (Primary key)\",\n      \"Integer (Primary Key)\",\n      \"Integer (Primary key )\",\n      \"Integer (Foreign key)\",\n      \"Integer (Foreign Key)\",\n      \"Integer\",\n      \"String (Foreign key)\",\n      \"String (Foreign Key)\",\n      \"String\",\n      \"Date & Time\",\n      \"Date\",\n      \"Boolean\",\n      \"Currency\",\n      \"Numeric /Decimal\",\n      \"Numeric / Decimal\",\n    ];\n\n    // Build a simpler approach: look for known column names\n    // Based on our reading of the full text, columns follow this pattern in the extracted text:\n    // ColumnName\\n\\nDataType\\n\\nDescription (optional)\\n\\nComments (optional)\n    // But pdftotext merges cells in unpredictable ways.\n\n    // Better approach: use the known file schemas to hard-code the structure\n    // since the PDF is well-structured and we've read it all.\n\n    // Actually, let's try a heuristic parse of the column blocks\n    const parsedColumns = parseColumnBlock(columnLines, dataTypes);\n\n    schemas.push({\n      filename,\n      folder,\n      contents_description: clean(contentsDesc),\n      columns: parsedColumns,\n    });\n  }\n\n  return schemas;\n}\n\nfunction parseColumnBlock(lines: string[], dataTypes: string[]): Column[] {\n  const columns: Column[] = [];\n\n  // Join all lines and split by known column name patterns\n  // Known column names that appear across schemas:\n  const knownColumnNames = new Set([\n    \"Patient ID\", \"Patient Account#\", We parse section by section.\n\nfunction parseSchemas(): FileSchema[] {\n  const schemas: FileSchema[] = [];\n\n  // Find all \"Filename:\" markers\n  const filenamePattern = /^Filename:\\s*(.+)/;\n  const contentsPattern = /^Contents:\\s*(.+)/;\n\n  let i = 0;\n  while (i < lines.length) {\n    const line = clean(lines[i]);\n\n    const fnMatch = line.match(filenamePattern);\n    if (!fnMatch) {\n      i++;\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    const filename = fnMatch[1].trim();\n    const folder = filename.split(\"/\")[0];\n    i++;\n\n    // Gather contents description (may span multiple lines)\n    let contentsDesc = \"\";\n    while (i < lines.length) {\n      const cl = clean(lines[i]);\n      if (!cl || cl.match(/^\\d+\\s*\\|\\s*P\\s*a\\s*g\\s*e/) || cl.match(/^Data Export/)) {\n        i++;\n        continue;\n      }\n      const cm = cl.match(contentsPattern);\n      if (cm) {\n        contentsDesc = cm[1];\n        i++;\n        // Continue gathering if next lines are continuation\n        while (i < lines.length) {\n          const nl = clean(lines[i]);\n          if (!nl || nl === \"Schema:\" || nl.match(/^Column Name/) || nl.match(/^\\d+\\s*\\|\\s*P\\s*a\\s*g\\s*e/) || nl.match(/^Data Export/)) break;\n          contentsDesc += \" \" + nl;\n          i++;\n        }\n        break;\n      }\n      break;\n    }\n\n    // Skip to \"Schema:\" or \"Column Name\"\n    while (i < lines.length) {\n      const cl = clean(lines[i]);\n      if (cl === \"Schema:\" || cl.match(/^Column Name/)) break;\n      i++;\n    }\n\n    // Skip \"Schema:\" line\n    if (clean(lines[i]) === \"Schema:\") i++;\n\n    // Skip \"Column Name\" header line and the \"Data Type\" / \"Description\" / \"Comments\" header\n    // The pdftotext output splits the 4-column table headers across lines\n    while (i < lines.length) {\n      const cl = clean(lines[i]);\n      if (cl === \"Column Name\" || cl === \"Data Type\" || cl === \"Description\" || cl === \"Comments\") {\n        i++;\n        continue;\n      }\n      break;\n    }\n\n    // Now parse columns. The pattern from pdftotext is:\n    // Column Name on its own line, then Data Type, then Description, then Comments\n    // Some values span multiple lines. We need to detect when a new column starts.\n    const columns: Column[] = [];\n    let currentCol: Partial<Column> | null = null;\n\n    // Collect all remaining lines until the next \"Filename:\" or end of known section\n    const columnLines: string[] = [];\n    while (i < lines.length) {\n      const cl = clean(lines[i]);\n      // Stop at next filename or at end markers\n      if (cl.match(/^Filename:/) || cl.match(/^PMS File Schemas/) || cl.match(/^EMR File Schemas/)) break;\n      // Skip page markers and data export footers\n      if (cl.match(/^\\d+\\s*\\|\\s*P\\s*a\\s*g\\s*e/) || cl.match(/^Data Export \\/ Version/)) {\n        i++;\n        continue;\n      }\n      columnLines.push(cl);\n      i++;\n    }\n\n    // Parse the column lines using knowledge of the data types that appear\n    const dataTypes = [\n      \"Integer (Primary key)\",\n      \"Integer (Primary Key)\",\n      \"Integer (Primary key )\",\n      \"Integer (Foreign key)\",\n      \"Integer (Foreign Key)\",\n      \"Integer\",\n      \"String (Foreign key)\",\n      \"String (Foreign Key)\",\n      \"String\",\n      \"Date & Time\",\n      \"Date\",\n      \"Boolean\",\n      \"Currency\",\n      \"Numeric /Decimal\",\n      \"Numeric / Decimal\",\n    ];\n\n    // Build a simpler approach: look for known column names\n    // Based on our reading of the full text, columns follow this pattern in the extracted text:\n    // ColumnName\\n\\nDataType\\n\\nDescription (optional)\\n\\nComments (optional)\n    // But pdftotext merges cells in unpredictable ways.\n\n    // Better approach: use the known file schemas to hard-code the structure\n    // since the PDF is well-structured and we've read it all.\n\n    // Actually, let's try a heuristic parse of the column blocks\n    const parsedColumns = parseColumnBlock(columnLines, dataTypes);\n\n    schemas.push({\n      filename,\n      folder,\n      contents_description: clean(contentsDesc),\n      columns: parsedColumns,\n    });\n  }\n\n  return schemas;\n}\n\nfunction parseColumnBlock(lines: string[], dataTypes: string[]): Column[] {\n  const columns: Column[] = No redirects. Last-Modified: Mon, 24 Mar 2025.\n\n2. **Fetched and examined the HTML page** — static HTML page (not SPA), no JavaScript required for content. The page is a transparency disclosure / compliance hub for GlaceEMR 6.0 containing:\n   - Certification statement and compliance certificate link\n   - Modules tested listing\n   - Pricing transparency section\n   - Real-world testing plans and results (2022–2024) — not relevant to EHI export\n   - **\"EHI Data Export\" section** with description of single-patient and population export capabilities\n   - Link to the data dictionary PDF\n\n3. **Found the EHI Data Export section** — located roughly 2/3 down the page, under an `<h1>` heading \"EHI Data Export\". The section describes:\n   - Single Patient Export: exports all EHI for one patient as a ZIP file\n   - Patient Population Export: exports EHI for selected patients or entire population as a ZIP\n   - Export format: ZIP containing CSV, HTML, PDF, JPG, PNG, XML files\n   - A single download link: **\"GlaceEMR Export Data Dictionary\"** pointing to `pdf/GlaceEMR Export Data Dictionary.pdf`\n\n4. **Downloaded the data dictionary PDF**:\n   ```bash\n   curl -sL 'https://docs.glaceemr.com/Glenwood/pdf/GlaceEMR%20Export%20Data%20Dictionary.pdf' -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o 'GlaceEMR Export Data Dictionary.pdf'\n   ```\n   Verified: `file` confirms \"PDF document, version 1.7, 62 page(s)\". 783,292 bytes.\n\n5. **Examined the PDF** using `pdfinfo` and `pdftotext`. No embedded URLs or attachments. Author: sraj, created with Microsoft Word for Microsoft 365, dated November 29, 2023.\n\n6. **Searched for additional files** — scanned the HTML source for all href links. Found only the data dictionary PDF and compliance certificate PDF as relevant downloads. No additional data dictionary files, schema files, or API documentation present on the page.\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### Export Mechanism\n\nGlaceEMR provides a genuine (b)(10) EHI export — **not** a repackaged FHIR/g(10) API. The export produces a ZIP file with a well-defined folder structure containing CSV data files, clinical documents in HTML/PDF format, scanned documents in their original formats, patient photos, C-CDA v2 XML documents, and a reference schema folder.\n\nThe export supports both single-patient and population-level operations.\n\n### Export Format\n\nThe ZIP file contains five top-level folders:\n\n| Folder | Contents |\n|--------|----------|\n| **EMR/** | 31 CSV files covering clinical data |\n| **PMS/** | 11 CSV files covering practice management/billing data |\n| **Documents/** | Clinical templates (HTML+PDF), scanned documents (PNG/JPEG), C-CDA v2 XML — organized per-patient |\n| **Photos/** | Patient photos in JPG format |\n| **Reference/** | Schema of the exported file content (details not elaborated) |\n\n### Data Dictionary Scope\n\nThe data dictionary documents **42 CSV files** totaling **820 columns** across the EMR and PMS folders. This is a CSV-based database dump covering both clinical and billing data.\n\n**EMR folder (31 files):**\n- Encounters, Vitals\n- 13 distinct history files: Past Medical, Surgical, Family, Family Relations, Social, Substance Abuse, Contraception/Sexual, Pregnancy, Birthing, Menstrual, Occupational, Obstetric, Exposure\n- Allergies (with NDC codes)\n- Assessments (with ICD-9/10, SNOMED, CPT4, NDC, LOINC, RXNORM coding)\n- Problem List (ICD-10, SNOMED)\n- Medications (active), Inactive Medications (with NDC)\n- Investigation (lab orders/results with diagnosis codes, CPT codes, result values and ranges)\n- Preventive Screenings\n- Coumadin dosage tracking (specialty-specific: INR/PT monitoring with daily dose schedules)\n- Vaccines (with CVX codes, manufacturer details, lot numbers)\n- Messages (phone/internal), Reminders\n- Index files for: Patient Photos, Clinical Documents, Phone Messages, Scans/Attachments, CDA Documents\n\n**PMS folder (11 files):**\n- Patient Demographics (comprehensive: name, address, contact, guarantor, emergency contact, race, ethnicity, language, pharmacy, employer, deceased status)\n- Patient Insurance (primary, secondary, tertiary — with subscriber details, copays)\n- Patient Account Balance\n- Transactions (procedure codes, charges, diagnosis pointers, payments, adjustments, balances)\n- Payment Receipts (cash/check/card/insurance with payment/adjustment/denial codes)\n- Payment Posting (linking receipts to services)\n- Appointments\n- Master lists: Insurance, Place of Service, Providers, Referring Providers\n\n### Relationships Between Files\n\nThe data dictionary explicitly documents foreign key relationships between tables:\n- Patient ID in all files references PMS/Patient_Demographics.csv\n- Encounter ID references EMR/Encounters.csv\n- Insurance IDs reference PMS/Master_Insurance_List.csv\n- Provider references to PMS/Master_Provider_List.csv and PMS/Master_Referring_Provider_List.csv\n- Receipt/Service ID cross-references between PMS payment files\n\n### Documents Folder Structure\n\nClinical documents are organized per-patient in folders named:\n`LastName,FirstName-(DOB MM-DD-YYYY)-(Account#)-[PatientID]`\n\nThree types of documents are exported:\n1. **Clinical Templates** — in both HTML and PDF formats, indexed by Clinical_Document_Index_File.csv\n2. **Scanned Documents/Attachments** — in original format (PNG/JPEG/etc.), indexed by Scan_And_Attachments_Index_File.csv\n3. **C-CDA v2 documents** — XML format, indexed by CDA_Document_Index_File.csv\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\n**Clearly covered domains:**\n- Demographics (comprehensive — PMS/Patient_Demographics.csv has 50+ fields)\n- Encounters and visit documentation\n- Vital signs\n- Extensive medical/social history (13 separate history categories — unusually granular)\n- Allergies with NDC coding\n- Problem lists and assessments with standard coding (ICD-9/10, SNOMED)\n- Active and inactive medications with NDC codes\n- Lab orders and results (Investigation.csv with result values, ranges, and status tracking)\n- Immunizations/vaccines with CVX codes and manufacturer details\n- Preventive screenings\n- Clinical documents (progress notes, specialty templates — exported as full HTML/PDF)\n- Scanned documents and attachments\n- C-CDA documents\n- Patient photos\n- Insurance information (primary, secondary, tertiary)\n- Billing transactions (charges, procedure codes, diagnosis pointers)\n- Payments and payment posting (with receipt details, denial codes, adjustment codes)\n- Account balances\n- Appointments\n- Phone/internal messages with doctor responses\n- Patient reminders\n- Coumadin/anticoagulation management (specialty clinical module)\n\n**Domains from product research with unclear or missing coverage:**\n\n- **E-Prescribing transaction data** — Medications are exported with NDC codes, but the actual e-prescribing transaction records (SureScripts messages, PDMP lookups, EPCS transactions) are not explicitly listed. The Medications.csv captures prescription content but may not capture the full prescribing workflow.\n\n- **Behavioral health specialty data** — The product research highlights a significant behavioral health module with PHQ-9, BIMS, Mini Mental Status Exam, DSM-5 assessments, treatment plans with goals, and group therapy management. The data dictionary does not have a dedicated behavioral health file. These assessments may be captured within the Clinical Document templates (exported as HTML/PDF) or within the generic Assessment/History files, but this isn't explicitly documented.\n\n- **Care coordination / CCM / RPM data** — Product research mentions Care Coordination Management and Remote Patient Monitoring modules. These are not represented by dedicated CSV files. Data may be in clinical documents.\n\n- **Patient portal messages** — The Messages.csv covers phone messages and internal messages. Patient portal two-way messaging is mentioned in the product research but not clearly mapped to a specific export file.\n\n- **Telehealth visit data** — The product mentions a comprehensive telemedicine module. Whether telehealth encounters appear in the standard Encounters.csv or have additional metadata isn't documented.\n\n- **Referrals** — The product is certified for (b)(7)-(b)(9) transitions of care, and the export includes referring doctor information. But dedicated referral records (referral requests, status, outcomes) don't have their own CSV.\n\n- **Care plans and goals** — The product research mentions treatment plans with goals and progress tracking. There's no dedicated care plan export file. Likely embedded in clinical documents.\n\n- **Growth charts** — Mentioned in product features; no dedicated export file.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export format is **CSV files in a ZIP archive** — a vendor-specific but highly practical format. This is appropriate for a (b)(10) export and far more comprehensive than a FHIR-only approach would be.\n\nKey format characteristics:\n- **CSV for structured data** — tabular, universally readable, no special software needed\n- **HTML + PDF for clinical documents** — dual format ensures both human readability and preservation\n- **C-CDA v2 XML** — standard clinical document format included alongside the CSV data\n- **Original format for attachments** — scanned documents exported in their stored format (PNG/JPEG)\n- **Standard coding systems** — ICD-9/10, SNOMED, NDC, CPT4, LOINC, RXNORM used where applicable\n\nRelationships between CSV files are documented through explicit foreign key references. A recipient could reconstruct patient records by following the documented key relationships: Patient_Demographics → Encounters → Vitals/Allergies/Medications/etc., with cross-references to master lists for providers and insurance.\n\n**Could a third party reconstruct the patient record?** Largely yes. The CSV files provide the structured data with standard codes, the clinical documents provide the full note content, and the relational structure is documented. The main gap is that some clinical content (specialty templates, behavioral health assessments) appears to be captured only within the HTML/PDF clinical documents rather than as structured CSV data, which limits computability for those domains.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\nThe data dictionary is **well-structured and reasonably detailed**:\n- Clear file-by-file organization with consistent format\n- Column names, data types, and descriptions for all fields\n- Foreign key relationships explicitly documented with cross-references\n- Possible values / value sets documented for coded fields (e.g., allergy types, encounter status, payment types, coding systems)\n- Consistent audit trail columns (Created By/On, Last Modified By/On) across all files\n- File naming conventions for documents folder clearly specified with examples\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- Many columns have empty Description and Comments fields — the column name is the only documentation\n- No worked examples or sample CSV rows\n- No formal schema files (no JSON Schema, XSD, or similar machine-readable constraint definitions) — though the \"Reference\" folder in the export is described as containing schemas\n- Data types are loosely specified (many fields are just \"String\" without length or format constraints)\n- No documentation of date/time formats used in the CSV files\n- No change history beyond Version 1.0\n- Version 1.0 dated November 2023 — no updates in 2+ years\n\n**Could a developer implement an import?** Yes, for the CSV data — the column names and types are clear enough to build a parser, and the foreign key relationships enable record linking. The clinical documents (HTML/PDF) would be harder to systematically import as structured data.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Granularity:** Field-level documentation for all 42 files. Column names, data types, and some constraints (possible values, foreign keys) are documented. Not all fields have descriptions.\n\n**Coded fields:** Value sets are partially documented. Some fields list possible values (e.g., Allergy Type, Encounter Status, Payment Type, Coding System names). Others (e.g., Encounter Type, Appointment Type) are left undocumented. The coding system names (ICD-9, ICD-10, SNOMED, etc.) are well-documented.\n\n**Relationships:** Foreign key relationships between entities are explicitly documented with references to the master file. This is a strong point — a recipient knows exactly how to link records across files.\n\n**Versioning:** Single version (1.0) with no change history beyond the initial release.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nGlaceEMR's EHI export represents a **genuine (b)(10) effort** that goes well beyond a FHIR-only approach. The export covers both clinical (EMR) and billing/practice management (PMS) data in a flat CSV format that is both comprehensive and accessible. The inclusion of 13 separate history categories, full billing transaction detail, payment posting, and clinical documents in their original formats demonstrates a commitment to exporting the complete designated record set.\n\nThe data dictionary is a serious document — 62 pages covering 42 files and 820 columns with foreign key relationships. It is clearly purpose-built for the (b)(10) requirement rather than repurposed from another certification criterion.\n\nThe main gaps are:\n1. **Specialty clinical data** (behavioral health assessments, treatment plans, care coordination) appears to be captured within clinical documents (HTML/PDF) rather than as structured CSV data. This means the data is exported but may require human reading rather than programmatic extraction.\n2. **Some product modules** (telehealth, patient portal messaging, RPM/CCM) lack explicit representation in the export documentation.\n3. **Documentation polish** — many column descriptions are empty, there are no sample files, and data format specifications (date formats, encoding) are missing.\n\nDespite these gaps, this is one of the more thorough (b)(10) implementations among small-to-mid-size EHR vendors. The vendor clearly understands the distinction between (b)(10) and (g)(10), and the CSV-based approach with a relational structure is pragmatic and appropriate.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://docs.glaceemr.com/Glenwood/transparencydisclosure.html\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (scroll to EHI section, click PDF link)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\nNone. Found only the data dictionary PDF and compliance certificate PDF as relevant downloads. No additional data dictionary files, schema files, or API documentation present on the page.\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### Export Mechanism\n\nGlaceEMR provides a genuine (b)(10) EHI export — **not** a repackaged FHIR/g(10) API. The export produces a ZIP file with a well-defined folder structure containing CSV data files, clinical documents in HTML/PDF format, scanned documents in their original formats, patient photos, C-CDA v2 XML documents, and a reference schema folder.\n\nThe export supports both single-patient and population-level operations.\n\n### Export Format\n\nThe ZIP file contains five top-level folders:\n\n| Folder | Contents |\n|--------|----------|\n| **EMR/** | 31 CSV files covering clinical data |\n| **PMS/** | 11 CSV files covering practice management/billing data |\n| **Documents/** | Clinical templates (HTML+PDF), scanned documents (PNG/JPEG), C-CDA v2 XML — organized per-patient |\n| **Photos/** | Patient photos in JPG format |\n| **Reference/** | Schema of the exported file content (details not elaborated) |\n\n### Data Dictionary Scope\n\nThe data dictionary documents **42 CSV files** totaling **820 columns** across the EMR and PMS folders. This is a CSV-based database dump covering both clinical and billing data.\n\n**EMR folder (31 files):**\n- Encounters, Vitals\n- 13 distinct history files: Past Medical, Surgical, Family, Family Relations, Social, Substance Abuse, Contraception/Sexual, Pregnancy, Birthing, Menstrual, Occupational, Obstetric, Exposure\n- Allergies (with NDC codes)\n- Assessments (with ICD-9/10, SNOMED, CPT4, NDC, LOINC, RXNORM coding)\n- Problem List (ICD-10, SNOMED)\n- Medications (active), Inactive Medications (with NDC)\n- Investigation (lab orders/results with diagnosis codes, CPT codes, result values and ranges)\n- Preventive Screenings\n- Coumadin dosage tracking (specialty-specific: INR/PT monitoring with daily dose schedules)\n- Vaccines (with CVX codes, manufacturer details, lot numbers)\n- Messages (phone/internal), Reminders\n- Index files for: Patient Photos, Clinical Documents, Phone Messages, Scans/Attachments, CDA Documents\n\n**PMS folder (11 files):**\n- Patient Demographics (comprehensive: name, address, contact, guarantor, emergency contact, race, ethnicity, language, pharmacy, employer, deceased status)\n- Patient Insurance (primary, secondary, tertiary — with subscriber details, copays)\n- Patient Account Balance\n- Transactions (procedure codes, charges, diagnosis pointers, payments, adjustments, balances)\n- Payment Receipts (cash/check/card/insurance with payment/adjustment/denial codes)\n- Payment Posting (linking receipts to services)\n- Appointments\n- Master lists: Insurance, Place of Service, Providers, Referring Providers\n\n### Relationships Between Files\n\nThe data dictionary explicitly documents foreign key relationships between tables:\n- Patient ID in all files references PMS/Patient_Demographics.csv\n- Encounter ID references EMR/Encounters.csv\n- Insurance IDs reference PMS/Master_Insurance_List.csv\n- Provider references to PMS/Master_Provider_List.csv and PMS/Master_Referring_Provider_List.csv\n- Receipt/Service ID cross-references between PMS payment files\n\n### Documents Folder Structure\n\nClinical documents are organized per-patient in folders named:\n`LastName,FirstName-(DOB MM-DD-YYYY)-(Account#)-[PatientID]`\n\nThree types of documents are exported:\n1. **Clinical Templates** — in both HTML and PDF formats, indexed by Clinical_Document_Index_File.csv\n2. **Scanned Documents/Attachments** — in original format (PNG/JPEG/etc.), indexed by Scan_And_Attachments_Index_File.csv\n3. **C-CDA v2 documents** — XML format, indexed by CDA_Document_Index_File.csv\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\n**Clearly covered domains:**\n- Demographics (comprehensive — PMS/Patient_Demographics.csv has 50+ fields)\n- Encounters and visit documentation\n- Vital signs\n- Extensive medical/social history (13 separate history categories — unusually granular)\n- Allergies with NDC coding\n- Problem lists and assessments with standard coding (ICD-9/10, SNOMED)\n- Active and inactive medications with NDC codes\n- Lab orders and results (Investigation.csv with result values, ranges, and status tracking)\n- Immunizations/vaccines with CVX codes and manufacturer details\n- Preventive screenings\n- Clinical documents (progress notes, specialty templates — exported as full HTML/PDF)\n- Scanned documents and attachments\n- C-CDA documents\n- Patient photos\n- Insurance information (primary, secondary, tertiary)\n- Billing transactions (charges, procedure codes, diagnosis pointers)\n- Payments and payment posting (with receipt details, denial codes, adjustment codes)\n- Account balances\n- Appointments\n- Phone/internal messages with doctor responses\n- Patient reminders\n- Coumadin/anticoagulation management (specialty clinical module)\n\n**Domains from product research with unclear or missing coverage:**\n\n- **E-Prescribing transaction data** — Medications are exported with NDC codes, but the actual e-prescribing transaction records (SureScripts messages, PDMP lookups, EPCS transactions) are not explicitly listed. The Medications.csv captures prescription content but may not capture the full prescribing workflow.\n\n- **Behavioral health specialty data** — The product research highlights a significant behavioral health module with PHQ-9, BIMS, Mini Mental Status Exam, DSM-5 assessments, treatment plans with goals, and group therapy management. The data dictionary does not have a dedicated behavioral health file. These assessments may be captured within the Clinical Document templates (exported as HTML/PDF) or within the generic Assessment/History files, but this isn't explicitly documented.\n\n- **Care coordination / CCM / RPM data** — Product research mentions Care Coordination Management and Remote Patient Monitoring modules. These are not represented by dedicated CSV files. Data may be in clinical documents.\n\n- **Patient portal messages** — The Messages.csv covers phone messages and internal messages. Patient portal two-way messaging is mentioned in the product research but not clearly mapped to a specific export file.\n\n- **Telehealth visit data** — The product mentions a comprehensive telemedicine module. Whether telehealth encounters appear in the standard Encounters.csv or have additional metadata isn't documented.\n\n- **Referrals** — The product is certified for (b)(7)-(b)(9) transitions of care, and the export includes referring doctor information. But dedicated referral records (referral requests, status, outcomes) don't have their own CSV.\n\n- **Care plans and goals** — The product research mentions treatment plans with goals and progress tracking. There's no dedicated care plan export file. Likely embedded in clinical documents.\n\n- **Growth charts** — Mentioned in product features; no dedicated export file.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export format is **CSV files in a ZIP archive** — a vendor-specific but highly practical format. This is appropriate for a (b)(10) export and far more comprehensive than a FHIR-only approach would be.\n\nKey format characteristics:\n- **CSV for structured data** — tabular, universally readable, no special software needed\n- **HTML + PDF for clinical documents** — dual format ensures both human readability and preservation\n- **C-CDA v2 XML** — standard clinical document format included alongside the CSV data\n- **Original format for attachments** — scanned documents exported in their stored format (PNG/JPEG)\n- **Standard coding systems** — ICD-9/10, SNOMED, NDC, CPT4, LOINC, RXNORM used where applicable\n\nRelationships between CSV files are documented through explicit foreign key references. A recipient could reconstruct patient records by following the documented key relationships: Patient_Demographics → Encounters → Vitals/Allergies/Medications/etc., with cross-references to master lists for providers and insurance.\n\n**Could a third party reconstruct the patient record?** Largely yes. The CSV files provide the structured data with standard codes, the clinical documents provide the full note content, and the relational structure is documented. The main gap is that some clinical content (specialty templates, behavioral health assessments) appears to be captured only within the HTML/PDF clinical documents rather than as structured CSV data, which limits computability for those domains.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\nThe data dictionary is **well-structured and reasonably detailed**:\n- Clear file-by-file organization with consistent format\n- Column names, data types, and descriptions for all fields\n- Foreign key relationships explicitly documented with cross-references\n- Possible values / value sets documented for coded fields (e.g., allergy types, encounter status, payment types, coding systems)\n- Consistent audit trail columns (Created By/On, Last Modified By/On) across all files\n- File naming conventions for documents folder clearly specified with examples\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- Many columns have empty Description and Comments fields — the column name is the only documentation\n- No worked examples or sample CSV rows\n- No formal schema files (no JSON Schema, XSD, or similar machine-readable constraint definitions) — though the \"Reference\" folder in the export is described as containing schemas\n- Data types are loosely specified (many fields are just \"String\" without length or format constraints)\n- No documentation of date/time formats used in the CSV files\n- No change history beyond Version 1.0\n- Version 1.0 dated November 2023 — no updates in 2+ years\n\n**Could a developer implement an import?** Yes, for the CSV data — the column names and types are clear enough to build a parser, and the foreign key relationships enable record linking. The clinical documents (HTML/PDF) would be harder to systematically import as structured data.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Granularity:** Field-level documentation for all 42 files. Column names, data types, and some constraints (possible values, foreign keys) are documented. Not all fields have descriptions.\n\n**Coded fields:** Value sets are partially documented. Some fields list possible values (e.g., Allergy Type, Encounter Status, Payment Type, Coding System names). Others (e.g., Encounter Type, Appointment Type) are left undocumented. The coding system names (ICD-9, ICD-10, SNOMED, etc.) are well-documented.\n\n**Relationships:** Foreign key relationships between entities are explicitly documented with references to the master file. This is a strong point — a recipient knows exactly how to link records across files.\n\n**Versioning:** Single version (1.0) with no change history beyond the initial release.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nGlaceEMR's EHI export represents a **genuine (b)(10) effort** that goes well beyond a FHIR-only approach. The export covers both clinical (EMR) and billing/practice management (PMS) data in a flat CSV format that is both comprehensive and accessible. The inclusion of 13 separate history categories, full billing transaction detail, payment posting, and clinical documents in their original formats demonstrates a commitment to exporting the complete designated record set.\n\nThe data dictionary is a serious document — 62 pages covering 42 files and 820 columns with foreign key relationships. It is clearly purpose-built for the (b)(10) requirement rather than repurposed from another certification criterion.\n\nThe main gaps are:\n1. **Specialty clinical data** (behavioral health assessments, treatment plans, care coordination) appears to be captured within clinical documents (HTML/PDF) rather than as structured CSV data. This means the data is exported but may require human reading rather than programmatic extraction.\n2. **Some product modules** (telehealth, patient portal messaging, RPM/CCM) lack explicit representation in the export documentation.\n3. **Documentation polish** — many column descriptions are empty, there are no sample files, and data format specifications (date formats, encoding) are missing.\n\nDespite these gaps, this is one of the more thorough (b)(10) implementations among small-to-mid-size EHR vendors. The vendor clearly understands the distinction between (b)(10) and (g)(10), and the CSV-based approach with a relational structure is pragmatic and appropriate.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://docs.glaceemr.com/Glenwood/transparencydisclosure.html\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (scroll to EHI section, click PDF link)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\nNone.
